...[if the left eyebrow should twitch, it indicates] to travel a road for good. But in other sources, it indicates toiling much and acquiring little. 

If the **upper eyelid of the left eye** should twitch, for a poor man it indicates acquisition and prosperity. For a slave, a plot. For a virgin, blame. For a widow, something good. For a rich man, an annoyance. For a farmer and a hunter, a benefit. For a soldier, advancement. 

If the **right corner of the left eye** <note>original: κανθὸς (kanthos); the corner of the eye where the lids meet</note> should twitch—that is, the corner near the nose—it indicates health and safety. 

If the **left corner [of the left eye]** should twitch, it indicates good things for everyone in this way. 

If the **lower eyelid of the left eye** should twitch, it signifies unpleasantness. For a slave, slander. For a virgin, purity <note>original: ἀφθαρσίαν (aphtharsian); literally "incorruptibility," suggesting her reputation or physical state remains untouched</note>. For a widow, an insult. 

If the **stinging corner** <note>original: κανθὸς ἰοβόλος (kanthos iobolos); likely referring to the outer corner of the eye, associated with "shooting" glances</note> **of the left eye** should twitch, it indicates unpleasantness and grief for everyone. For a slave, a benefit. For a virgin, disease. 

If the **tail** <note>original: οὐραία (ouraia); the outer edge or corner of the eye</note> **of the left eye** should twitch, it indicates many good things. For slaves, the attainment of trust, from which they will benefit. For a virgin, blame. For a widow, grief. :~

If the **right part of the nose** should twitch, it indicates mental unpleasantness. For a slave, a benefit. For a virgin, counsel. For a widow, submission. 

If the **left part of the nose** should twitch, it indicates unexpected good things. For a slave, a benefit. For a virgin, counsel. For a widow, submission. 

If the **middle of the nose** should twitch, it indicates grief. For a slave, a critical moment <note>original: ἀκμήν (akmen); a peak or turning point in one's fortunes or health</note>. For a virgin, marriage. For a widow, blame. 

If the **nose should twitch all over**, it indicates unexpected good things in every respect. 

If the **tip of the left part of the nose** should twitch, it indicates loss. For a slave, hardship. For a virgin, blame. For a widow, slander. :~

If the **right nostril** should twitch, it indicates a benefit; but for a slave, a virgin, and a widow, it indicates unpleasantness. 

If the **left nostril** should twitch, it indicates a benefit. For a slave, a change of household. For a virgin, a husband. For a widow, a benefit. 

If the **middle of the nostril** should twitch, it indicates loss and insult. For a slave, joy. For a widow, a benefit. :~

If the **right cheek** <note>original: Μῆλον (melon); literally "apple," referring to the fleshy part of the cheek or cheekbone</note> should twitch, it indicates an unexpected insult. For a slave, something good. For a virgin, an insult. 

If the **left cheek** should twitch, it indicates good in every respect. For a slave, a long journey abroad. For a virgin, blame. For a widow, a benefit. 

If the **upper lip** should twitch, it signifies a dispute with someone and victory. For a slave, something good. For a virgin, profit. 

If the **lower lip** should twitch, it indicates a benefit, or the loss of a task. :~

If the **right jaw** should twitch, it indicates a benefit. For a slave, to be entrusted with the management of the house. For a virgin, disease. For a widow, blame. 

If the **left jaw** should twitch...

It reflects a world where minor bodily twitches were seen as divine signals of social advancement, legal disputes, or personal health.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the "Palmoskopia" (divination by muscle twitches), a traditional manual often attributed to the legendary seer Melampus. While the previous page (101) discussed the eyes, we have skipped ahead to page 107, which covers the jaw, ears, chin, palate, heart, and neck. The text maintains its rigid structure of interpreting "palms" (twitches) based on the person's social status—slave, poor man, widow, or soldier.</meta>

<page-num>107</page-num>

...[this] indicates grief. If it is a small matter: for a slave, a benefit; for a poor man <note>original: πενένω (peneno); likely a corruption of the Greek word for a poor person</note>, fear; for a widow, joy. ~

**The jaw** <note>original: Γνάθος (Gnathos)</note>, when the right side twitches, indicates joy. For a slave, profit. For a poor man, counsel. For a widow, a benefit. When the left jaw twitches, it indicates hardship. For a slave, disease. For a poor man, an insult. For a widow, unpleasantness. ~

**The ear** <note>original: Ὠτίον (Otion)</note>, when the right one twitches, or if there is a ringing, it signifies joy over something. For a slave, joy. For a poor man, an acquisition. For a widow, something good. When the left ear twitches, it indicates that something lofty will happen. For a slave, the undertaking of great matters. For a poor man, fear. For a widow, deprivation. When the inner part of the right ear twitches, it signifies something bad. When the outer part of the left ear twitches, it signifies something bad—[yet] he will hear of it or rejoice. But for a slave, it is good. ~

**The chin** <note>original: Γενείου (Geneiou)</note>, when the right side twitches, it indicates a good acquisition for everyone. When the left side of the chin twitches, it indicates good hopes for everyone, and is good for all. When the lip twitches, it indicates talkativeness. ~

**The palate** <note>original: Οὐρανίσκος (Ouraniskos); literally "the little heaven," the roof of the mouth</note>, when twitching, indicates joy, and also loss. But the "unnamed" <note>original: ὠνώνυμον (ononymon); a common euphemism in these manuals for the left side</note> side indicates something good. If the windpipe <note>original: βρηχὸς (brechos); likely for βρόγχος (bronchos)</note> should twitch, it indicates something good for both slave and free man. If the **teeth** clash together with twitches, they indicate something good. If the **mouth** should twitch, he will see someone, but after a long time, and he will rejoice at it. **The heart** <note>original: Καρδία (Kardia)</note>, if it should twitch, indicates something good for everyone. For others, cheerfulness. For a slave, freedom. For a poor man, sluggishness. For a widow and a soldier, joy. For a merchant, business. For others, a treacherous friend is indicated. ~

**The neck** <note>original: Τραχήλου (Trachelou); usually the front or sides of the neck</note>, if the right side should twitch: for a free man, fear; but for a slave, it signifies disease. For all others, it is good. If the left side of the neck should twitch, it is good. If the right side of the **throat** <note>original: φάρυγγος (pharyngos)</note> should twitch, it indicates joy. For a slave and a free man, profit. When the left side of the throat twitches, it indicates an escape from unpleasantness for everyone. For others, it signifies "free glory" <note>original: ἐλευθέραν δόξαν (eleutheran doxan); perhaps a reputation gained without effort</note>. For a slave, bad news. For a soldier, the acquisition of money. For a man in need, a way out. From female persons: for a virgin, unpleasantness. For a ship's captain, praise. **The nape of the neck** <note>original: Αὐχὴν (Auchen); the back of the neck</note>, if the right side should twitch, indicates something good. For a slave, worries. For a poor man, a remembrance. For a widow, pregnancy <note>original: κύην (kyen)</note>. For a soldier, freedom from worry. But the "unnamed" side indicates something wicked. For a slave, an ornament. For a poor man, a breeze. For a widow, joy. For a slave, something good. For others, it is not good. For others, it indicates a plot. But for one who is in distress, it will release his evils. For bad slaves, disease. For a virgin, a lively annoyance. For a soldier, tears. For a merchant, a repayment. For a ship's captain...

<summary>This page continues a divinatory manual interpreting muscle twitches in the jaw, ears, mouth, and neck. It highlights how the same physical sensation could be interpreted as "freedom" for a slave but "joy" or "fear" for other social classes.</summary>

--- Page 235 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Palmoskopia," a Byzantine-era system of divination through muscle twitches (palmoi). The previous page concluded with the palate. This page transitions through the neck/throat area and moves systematically down the arm, from the shoulder to the thumb, maintaining the traditional breakdown of what these involuntary movements mean for different social classes—from slaves and widows to merchants and prominent citizens.</meta>

...abundance. The left side <note>original: εὐώνυμος (euonymos); literally "the well-named," a euphemism for the left side, which was often considered unlucky</note> indicates unpleasantness for a free man, but for a slave, it indicates disease. For a widow, it suggests something expected. For a soldier, it indicates demotion. For a merchant, it signifies loss. For a ship’s pilot, it indicates grief. For a woman, it signifies unpleasantness. For prominent men <note>original: δήλοις (delois); referring to notable or distinguished citizens</note>, it indicates a loss for travelers. For a slave, it signifies griefs. But for all others, it is good; for everyone else, it indicates feasting.

---

<margin>Ω</margin> <note>The letter Omega (Ω) stands for "Omos," the shoulder.</note> **The right shoulder** <note>original: Ωμος δεξιὸς (Omos dexios)</note>, when it twitches, indicates some benefit. For a craftsman, it indicates attainment in his work. For slaves, it signifies a benefit, and even the death of their master <note>interpreted historically as a path to potential freedom or change in status</note>. For a poor man, it indicates marriage. For a widow, profit. For merchants, a benefit. For a ship’s pilot, abundance. For a woman, joy. **The left shoulder** <note>original: Ωμος λαιὸς (Omos laios)</note>, when it twitches, signifies a plot by a woman—yet it will be a beneficial one.

---

<margin>Β</margin> <note>The letter Beta (Β) stands for "Brachion," the upper arm.</note> **The right upper arm** <note>original: Βραχίων δεξιὸς (Brachion dexios)</note>, when it twitches, indicates the acquisition of children and money. For moneylenders, it indicates harm from others. For a prominent man, children will be under his authority. For a slave, it signifies great trust in his affairs. For a poor man, submission. For a widow, a benefit. **The left upper arm**, when it twitches, signifies assistance from members of one’s own household. For a slave, it indicates freedom. For a prominent man, it indicates grief.

---

<margin>Μ</margin> <note>The letter Mu (Μ) stands for "Mys," the muscle or bicep.</note> **The right bicep**, when it twitches, signifies loss for everyone. But **the left bicep** indicates unexpected profit.

---

<margin>Α</margin> <note>The letter Alpha (Α) stands for "Ankon," the elbow.</note> **The right elbow**, when it twitches, indicates a benefit. For a slave, it means being delivered from evils. For a poor man, fear. For a widow, an insult. **The left elbow** indicates a loss on behalf of some person. For prominent men, it indicates cheer. For a slave, progress. For a poor man, fear. For a widow, an insult.

---

<margin>Π</margin> <note>The letter Pi (Π) stands for "Pechys," the forearm.</note> **The right forearm**, when it twitches, indicates harm. For a slave, cheer. For a poor man, unprofitability. For a widow, unpleasantness. **The left forearm** indicates unpleasantness. For a slave, abundance. For a poor man, cheer; and likewise for a widow.

---

<margin>Χ</margin> <note>The letter Chi (Χ) stands for "Cheir," the hand.</note> **The right hand**, when it twitches, signifies a benefit. **The left hand** indicates the keeping of a trust. **The right wrist** <note>original: καρπὸς δεξιὸς (karpos dexios)</note>, when it twitches, signifies the repayment of money that is owed. It also indicates that one will be in poverty, though only for a short time. Then, it shows for all: if for prominent men, it indicates something bad. For a slave, harshness. For a poor man, marriage. For a widow, a journey abroad. **The left wrist** signifies a plot on account of business matters, but later a benefit. For prominent men, it indicates sickness. For a slave, something good. For a poor man, counsel. For a widow, a benefit. **The right thumb** <note>original: ἀντίχειρ ὁ δεξιὸς (anticheir ho dexios)</note>, when it twitches, indicates something good. For a slave, cheer. For a poor man, marriage. For a widow, a seizure <note>original: ἀρπαγήν (arpagen); can mean abduction or a sudden grasping of property</note>. **The left thumb** indicates a loss of livelihood. For a slave, freedom. For a poor man, an insult. For a widow, a benefit.

<summary>This page details the divinatory meanings of muscle twitches in the upper limbs, spanning from the shoulder to the thumb. The interpretations vary significantly based on the individual's social standing, such as promising freedom to slaves or marriage to the poor.</summary>
<keywords>Palmoskopia, divination, Byzantine medicine, anatomy, shoulder, arm, hand, social status, folklore, omens</keywords>

--- Page 236 ---
<meta>This page continues the systematic catalog of muscle twitches (palmoi) and their divinatory meanings. The previous page discussed the shoulders and upper arms; this page continues with the sides of the hand, the palms, the shoulder blades, and begins a detailed analysis of the fingers, incorporating planetary associations for the digits.</meta>

If the **side of the hand** twitches, it indicates that one will fall into certain laborious matters. For a **slave**, it indicates abundance. For a **maiden**, something good. For a **widow**, something evil. If it occurs in those who are traveling <note>original: τοῖς ἔξω (tois exo); literally "those outside," usually referring to travelers or those away from home</note>, it indicates a dispersion of voluntary affairs. If the side of the **left hand** twitches, it indicates enslavement by men. For a **slave**, it suggests a change in his way of life. For a **maiden**, something good; for a **widow**, gladness. For others, it signifies some form of profit.

If the **middle of the right hand**, that is to say the **hollow of the palm**, twitches, it signifies a benefit. It also indicates profit arising from the benefit of another person's business. For a **slave**, it indicates unpleasantness. For a **maiden**, it suggests she will receive advice. For a **widow**, profit. 

Twitching of the **left palm** <note>original: εὐωνύμου (euonymou); "the well-named," a euphemism for the left side</note> indicates great good fortune and mastery over enemies. For others, it indicates dominion over enemies. For a **slave**, it suggests freedom from care. For a **maiden**, it indicates an insult <note>original: ὕβριν (hybrin); can mean an assault, an insult, or a violation of status</note> followed by marriage. For a **widow**, an insult. 

If the **top of the right hand** <note>the back of the hand</note> twitches, it signifies something wicked. For a **slave**, a delay. For a **maiden**, unpleasantness. For a **widow**, a benefit. For others, it signifies an unexpected good. 

If the **right side of the left shoulder blade** <term>omoplates; the scapula</term> twitches, it signifies something good. For a **slave**, a change. For a **maiden**, fear. For a **widow**, salvation from a disease. For others, it indicates an outgrowth of indifference <note>original: ἔκφυσις ἀκηδίας (ekphysis akedias); a strange phrasing suggesting a sudden release or manifestation of lethargy or spiritual distress</note>. For **women**, it indicates instability. For a **slave**, rest from slavery. For a **maiden**, unpleasantness. For a **widow**, fear. For a **soldier**, tears. For **merchants**, a repayment. For a **ship's pilot**, a fair voyage.

If the **middle of the shoulder** twitches, it indicates the mastery of many good things, for both slave and free man alike. 

If the **left shoulder blade** twitches, and it is among the [unclear], it signifies folly. For those who lend money, it means the repayment of debts. For **slaves and thieves**, it signifies a relocation. For a **maiden**, sluggishness. For a **widow**, danger. For a **soldier**, gladness accompanied by profit. For all others, toil. 

If the **middle between the shoulder blades** twitches, it indicates abundance. For a **slave**, hardship and ingratitude. For a **maiden**, an insult. For a **widow**, slander. 

If the **right acromion** <term>akromion; the highest point of the shoulder</term> twitches, it indicates freedom and a healthy temperament. For a **slave**, a rest from evils. For a **maiden**, a betrothal. For a **widow**, cheerfulness. For a **soldier**, danger.

If the **little finger of the right hand** twitches, it indicates that the person will be deemed worthy of a greater trust, because this is the **finger of Hermes** <note>original: ἑρμοῦ (Hermou); Mercury, the god of commerce, communication, and boundaries</note>. For others, it indicates barrenness. For a **slave**, slander. For a **maiden**, fear. For a **widow**, an insult. 

If the **second finger after the little one** on the right hand twitches—the one called the **ring finger** <term>paramesos; the finger next to the middle</term>—it signifies the mastery of much wealth, for this is the **finger of the Sun** <note>original: ἡλίου (heliou); Helios</note>. For others, it indicates the acquisition of many good things. For a **slave**, evil. For a **maiden**, advice. For a **widow**, gladness.

<summary>This page details the divinatory meanings of twitches in the hands, palms, and shoulders, specifically noting how outcomes differ by social class and introducing planetary associations for the fingers.</summary>
<keywords>Palmoskopia, divination, omens, Hermes, Helios, shoulder blades, social classes, Byzantine medicine</keywords>

--- Page 237 ---
<meta>This page continues the catalog of "palmoi" (muscle twitches), moving from the back of the hand to a specific digit-by-digit analysis of the fingers and nails, then proceeding to the armpits, breasts, and torso.</meta>

The **third finger of the right hand**, which is to say the **middle finger**, indicates the evil eye <note>original: βασκανίαν (baskanian); the influence of envy or a malevolent glance</note> and insults. For this is the **finger of Saturn** <note>original: κρόνου (kronou). In ancient palmistry, the middle finger was associated with Saturn/Kronos, representing discipline or misfortune.</note>. In other traditions, it indicates that glory will be achieved. For a **slave**, a pleasant scent <note>original: εὐωδια (euodia); perhaps implying an offering or good news</note> arises. For a **poor man**, disease. For a **widow**, profit. ~

The **fourth finger** <note>the ring finger</note> twitching indicates loss accompanied by insult. For this is the **finger of Aphrodite** <note>original: ἀφροδίτης (aphrodites). The text assigns the ring finger and the thumb to Aphrodite, though traditions vary.</note>. In other traditions, it indicates harm. For a **free person**, a cutting-off <note>original: ἐκκοπιν (ekkopin); could mean a literal injury or being "cut off" from status</note>. For a **poor man**, profit. For a **widow**, benefit. ~

The **great finger**, which is also called the **thumb**, twitching indicates the acquisition of many good things. He will rejoice over both children and a wife. For this is also the **finger of Aphrodite**. For others, it indicates a release. For a **slave**, something good. For a **poor man**, a favorable breeze <note>original: αὔρα (aura); a metaphor for a change in fortune</note>. For a **widow**, benefit. ~

If the **nails of the right hand** twitch, they indicate unexpected profit. For a **slave**, gladness. For a **poor man**, a betrothal. If the **nail of the little finger of the right hand** twitches, or has a "crawling" sensation <note>original: μυρμηκιᾷ (myrmekia); literally "like ants," a tingling sensation</note>, it indicates suffering. For a **slave**, a child. For a **poor man**, a favorable breeze. For a **widow**, pregnancy. ~

If the nail of the **second finger** <note>the index finger</note> twitches, it indicates authority. For a **slave**, the death of his master. For a **poor man**, a reproach <note>original: ψόφον (psophon); literally a noise or sound, often meaning a rumor or social censure</note>. For a **widow**, something good. The nails of the **third** and **fourth** fingers indicate griefs. For a **slave**, something good. The nail of the **great finger** indicates an increase in livelihood.

The **little finger of the left hand** twitching indicates many good things for everyone. The **second finger** of that same hand, being accusatory <note>original: ἐγκληματικός (enklimatikos); likely referring to the finger used for pointing</note>, indicates grief and a defense regarding great downfalls. For a **slave in a position of trust**, an accusation. The **middle finger** indicates grief because of a personal matter. It also signifies an increase in livelihood, children, and money. The **great finger of the left hand** signifies recovering a past loss. ~

**Nails of the left hand** twitching indicate benefit, though with some difficulty. ~

The **right armpit** twitching indicates fights and griefs coming from one's own relatives for a short time for everyone, and the **left armpit** signifies the same. ~

The **right breast** twitching indicates a great [benefit] for a **poor man**. In other traditions, it indicates gladness. The **left breast** twitching signifies wealth. The **middle of the breast** <note>the sternum or chest center</note> twitching is a sign of virtuous concerns. ~

The **chest** twitching indicates a feast for everyone; in other traditions, the mind will be filled with fear. The **heart** twitching indicates a plot by a friend. ~

The **stomach** <note>original: Γαστὴρ (Gaster); the upper stomach/belly</note> twitching, for a **rich man**, indicates household expenses. For a **poor man**, luxury. For others, it indicates gladness. ~

The **belly** <note>original: Κοιλία (Koilia); the lower abdomen or gut</note> twitching signifies good things. For others, it indicates children who bring benefit.

<summary>This page details the prognostic meanings of twitches in the fingers, nails, armpits, and chest area, often differentiating outcomes by social class (slave, poor man, widow). It notably incorporates planetary associations, linking the fingers to Saturn and Aphrodite.</summary>
<keywords>fingers, nails, palmomancy, planetary influence, Saturn, Aphrodite, social status, pregnancy, heart, stomach, divination</keywords>

--- Page 238 ---
<meta>This page continues the divinatory catalog of "palmoi" (muscle twitches), moving from the hands and digits described on the previous page down through the torso, the reproductive organs, and the upper legs.</meta>

If the **right part of the belly** twitches, it indicates a disease of short duration. If the **right flank** twitches, it indicates a loss of dignity <note>original: ἀσχημίαν (aschemian); a loss of form, grace, or social standing</note>. In other traditions, it signifies many good things. If the **left flank** twitches, it indicates joy for everyone. In other traditions, it indicates work <note>original: ἐργασίαν (ergasian); can also mean business or gainful employment</note>. ~

If the **right side** <note>the rib area</note> twitches, it indicates long-term poverty for a **rich man**. For a **slave**, it indicates abundance and joy. In other traditions, it indicates benefit. If the **left side** twitches, it indicates comfort in all of life for one who is suffering <note>original: μοχθήζοντι (mochthezonti); one who is burdened by hard labor or distress</note>. If the **spleen** twitches, it indicates weakness <note>original: ἀῤῥωσίαν (arrhostian); a lack of strength or a state of ill health</note>. ~

If the **liver** twitches, it signifies despondency. If the **right part of the hip** twitches, it concerns relatives and friends. In another tradition, it indicates something good. If the **left part of the hip** twitches, it indicates sorrows. In another tradition, it indicates instability. ~

<margin>Spine</margin> If the **right parts of the spine** twitch, it indicates to everyone that they will prevail over their enemies.

<margin>Back</margin> If the **right back** twitches, it indicates an insult. The **left back**, for a **rich man**, indicates a respite <note>original: ἀνωχίαν (anochian); a pause, truce, or cessation of troubles</note>. For a **poor man**, it indicates labor. ~

<margin>Glans</margin> If the **glans of the penis** twitches, it indicates for a **slave** that he will come to Aphrodite <note>original: εἰς ἀφροδίτην (eis aphroditen); a euphemism for sexual activity or finding love</note>. If the **foreskin** twitches, it signifies a pleasure that is believed <note>or "trusted to happen"</note>. ~

<margin>Testicle</margin> If the **right testicle** twitches, it indicates the acquisition of a livelihood and children. If the **left testicle** twitches, it indicates feasting and benefit from a wife. ~

<margin>Buttock</margin> If the **right buttock** <note>original: Πυγὴ (pyge)</note> twitches, it indicates business and work from the outside that brings envy. If the **left buttock** twitches, it indicates expenses for a **rich man**. For the rest, it indicates something good; in other traditions, it means they will gain mastery over future things.

If a **finger** <note>original: δάκτυλος (daktylos); could also refer to a toe, given the anatomical progression down the body</note> twitches, it indicates unexpected profit.

If the **right hip-socket** <note>original: κοτύλη (kotyle)</note> twitches, it indicates movement toward progress for everyone. In other traditions, it signifies something good. If the **left hip-socket** twitches, it indicates release from some sorrow. In other traditions, it signifies unpleasantness. ~

<margin>Buttock</margin> If the **right buttock** <note>original: Γλουτός (gloutos)</note> twitches, it indicates abundance; and the **left** indicates the same.

<margin>Groin</margin> If the **right groin** twitches, it indicates violence and disturbances. If the **left groin** twitches, it signifies something not good. ~

<margin>Thigh</margin> If the **right thigh** twitches, it indicates benefit. In other traditions, it indicates mastery over enemies. If the **left thigh** twitches, it indicates a plot and deceit. In other traditions, it indicates a journey that will be beneficial to undertake. ~

<margin>Back of the thigh</margin> If the **right back of the thigh** <note>the hamstring area</note> twitches, it foretells blame. In other traditions, it indicates nobility <note>original: καλοκαγαθίαν (kalokagathian); the classical Greek ideal of gentlemanly goodness and beauty</note>. If the **left back of the thigh** twitches, it indicates a plot. For others, it indicates abundance. ~

<margin>Knee</margin> If the **right knee** twitches, it indicates feasting for everyone. For others, it indicates abundance.

<summary>This page catalogs the divinatory meanings of muscle spasms (palmoi) in the torso, reproductive organs, and upper legs. The interpretations reflect ancient social structures, offering distinct omens for the wealthy, the poor, and the enslaved.</summary>
<keywords>palmoskopia, divination, anatomy, liver, spleen, genitals, social class, omens, ancient Greek medicine, Melampus</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the divinatory guide to "palmoi" (muscle twitches), moving from the lower torso and genitals discussed on the previous page down the legs to the soles of the feet and the toenails.</meta>

If the **left knee** twitches, it indicates great unpleasantness. ~
**The right bend of the knee** <note>original: ἀγκύλη (ankylē); the popliteal space or the "hook" of the knee</note> twitching indicates unpleasantness. But the **left** indicates joy. If the **right shin** twitches, it indicates something notable <note>original: ἐπίσημον (episēmon); can mean a distinguished person or an official sign</note> or that a long journey must be traveled. The **left**, however, indicates sorrow for men, but for women, blame. To others, it indicates a period of illness. ~
If the **right ankle** twitches, it indicates benefit. The **left** indicates that one will be victorious in a court of law. ~
If the **right front of the leg** <note>the area over the tibia or shin-bone</note> twitches, it indicates prosperity. The **left** indicates that there will be a loss of property, or it indicates something wicked. ~
If the **right calf** <note>original: γαστροκνήμιον (gastroknēmion); literally "the belly of the leg"</note> twitches, it indicates unexpected good things for everyone. The **left** indicates an obstacle to a planned journey. ~
If the **right hamstring** <note>original: κορκὶς (korkis); a term for the tendons or muscles at the back of the leg</note> twitches, it indicates sorrow. The **left** indicates a profitable journey. ~
If the **ankle-bone of the right foot** twitches, it indicates anxiety or unpleasantness. The **left** indicates the same. ~
If the **right heel** twitches, it indicates attaining prosperity. And the **left** indicates the same. Likewise also for the **arch** <note>original: κοῖλον (koilon); the hollow part</note> **of both feet**. But for the **left arch**, it also indicates something beautiful. If the **arch of the right foot** twitches, it clearly indicates a journey to be traveled that is beneficial to the soul, and to tread back where others have not entered <note>this likely refers to reaching a place of unique or spiritual importance</note> indicates something else. ~
If the **right instep** <note>original: ταρσὸς (tarsos); the upper surface of the foot</note> twitches, it indicates something good; the **left** indicates joy. ~
If the **right sole** <note>original: τέλμα (telma)</note> should twitch, it indicates a journey to be traveled. The **left** signifies a long-term trip abroad. ~
If the **outer side of the right foot’s step** twitches, it indicates safety. The **left** indicates joy. ~
If the **little toe of the right foot** twitches, it indicates benefit. The **second toe** indicates work. The **third and middle toe** signifies something good; for a **slave**, a journey abroad; for a **poor man**, an insult; for a **widow**, a feast. And the **toe after it** <note>the fourth toe</note> indicates a journey abroad; for a **slave** and a **poor man**, harm; for a **widow**, sickness. In other traditions, it indicates benefit. The **big toe** indicates a journey abroad; for a **slave**, a stable condition; for a **poor man**, marriage; for a **widow**, cheerfulness. ~
If the **nails of the right foot** twitch, or if they tingle <note>original: μυρμηκιῶσι (myrmēkiōsi); to feel a sensation like ants crawling on the skin</note>, it indicates being somewhat weakened <note>original: μαλακισθῆναι (malakisthēnai); often implies physical illness or a loss of masculine vigor</note>. For a **slave**, it indicates strength. For a **poor man** and a **widow**, blame. The **nail of the little toe** indicates something good. That of the **second toe**, a good journey abroad. That of the **third**, unpleasantness. That of the **fourth**, benefit. That of the **fifth**...

...indicates something good. ~
If the **little toe of the left foot** twitches, it signifies a good thing.
For a **slave**, a change of masters. For a **poor man** <note>original: πένενι (peneni); a variant of the Greek for a laborer or person of modest means</note>, an insult. For a **widow**, joy. The **second toe** indicates a good journey abroad. For a **slave**, joy. For a **poor man**, counsel. For a **widow**, submission. The **middle toe** indicates unpleasantness. For a **slave**, a benefit. For a **poor man**, sluggishness. For a **widow**, unpleasantness. The **fourth toe** indicates benefit from friends. For a **slave**, disease. For a **poor man**, marriage. For a **widow**, slander. The **big toe** signifies the greatest good things. For a **slave**, prosperity. For a **poor man**, marriage. For a **widow**, benefit. ~
If the **nails of the left foot** twitch or <term>tingle</term> <note>original: μύρμηκιῶντες (myrmēkiōntes); literally "acting like ants," describing a "pins and needles" or creeping sensation</note>, for a **rich man**, they signify unpleasantness. But for a **poor man**, wealth. For a **slave**, harm. For a **poor man**, counsel. For a **widow**, blame. ~
If the **whole body** twitches, it indicates that one should be on guard for some days; for a **slave**, not to be plotted against. For a **poor man**, not to be in danger. For a **widow**, not to fall ill. ~

--- Page 241 ---
<meta>This page transitions from "palmoscopy" (the interpretation of muscle twitches) to "moleosophy," a form of divination based on the location of moles or birthmarks on the body. It maintains the previous page's interest in predicting a person's fortune, marriage prospects, and character based on physical signs.</meta>

# CONCERNING MOLES ON THE BODY <note>original: ΠΕΡΙ ΕΛΑΙΩΝ ΤΟΥ ΣΩΜΑΤΟΣ (Peri elaion tou somatos); while 'elaia' literally means 'olive,' in this context it refers to a mole or birthmark</note>

**If a man has one on his forehead**, he will be the master of many good things. If it is on a **woman’s forehead**, the woman will rule or be very great. If it is **outside the eyebrows of a man**, he will take a good and beautiful wife. If on a **woman’s**, and her complexion is **reddish** <note>original: πυρρά (pyrra)</note>, she will take a rich and handsome husband. If it is **on the eyebrows of a man**, one should not marry him, because he will become the husband of five women. The same applies to a woman.

If it is on the **man’s nose** and his complexion is **reddish**, he will be a man of sexual passion <note>original: τῆς συνουσίας (tēs synousias)</note>, because he also has a mole in a **hidden place** <note>original: εἰς τὸ κρυπτὸν (eis to krypton); a common euphemism in these texts for the genitals</note>. If it is on the **nose or the eye of a woman**, she will be led to a husband in the same way, because she too has a mole in a hidden place. If it is on the **width of a man’s nose**, he will travel abroad from country to country. On a **woman**, she will be **unlucky** <note>original: κακοποδίνα (kakopodina); literally 'evil-footed,' meaning one who brings or experiences bad luck</note>, because she also has a mole in a hidden place.

If it is on the **man’s cheek**, he will become rich. On a **woman**, if it is on the **lower part of the jaw** <note>original: σιαγώνος (siagōnos)</note>, she will be passionate, because she also has a mole on her **lower belly** <note>original: ὑπογάστριον (hypogastrion)</note>. If it is on the **man’s tongue**, he will take a rich and beautiful wife. If there is a mole on the **man’s lips**, he will be a glutton <note>original: πολύφαγος (polyphagos)</note>; likewise also for women. 

If a man has a mole on his **beard**, he will be rich in gold and silver; the same for a woman, because she also has one on the **groove** <note>original: αὐλῶνα (aulōna); literally a 'furrow' or 'channel,' likely referring to the vulva or the perineum</note>. If a man has it on his **ears**, he will be rich and blessed; the same for a woman, because she also has a mole on her **thigh**. If a man has it on his **throat**, he will be exceedingly rich; likewise also for a woman. 

If a man has it on the **nape of the neck** behind him, he will be beheaded. If on the **shoulders**, he will be poor and unlucky, carrying burdens, and the same for a woman. If on the **shoulders**, he will be a prisoner and oppressed. If in the **armpit** <note>original: μάσλην (maslēn)</note>, he will take a rich and beautiful wife, and the woman likewise. If on the **hands**, he will have many children, and the woman likewise. 

If on the **chest**, he will be a pauper, and the woman likewise. If it is **above the heart**, he will be very wicked; for a woman, this happens when she has the mole on her **breast** <note>original: μασθόν (masthon)</note>. If on the **stomach**, they will both be gluttons. If on the **groove**, they will be passionate and sickly. If on the **lower belly**, they will be passionate. If on the...

<summary>This text belongs to the tradition of "moleosophy," providing a catalog of moles and their divinatory meanings. It frequently links visible moles on the face or neck to "hidden" moles on the genitals or lower body to predict a person's sexual character and financial future.</summary>

<keywords>moleosophy, divination, physiognomy, moles, marriage omens, wealth, Greek medicine, folk belief</keywords>

--- Page 242 ---
<meta>This page concludes the section on moleosophy (divination by moles), continuing the previous page's anatomical progression from the face and torso down to the lower extremities and private parts. It ends with a general rule for interpreting the right and left sides of the body.</meta>

...hidden <note>original: κρυπτόν (krypton); referring to the genitals as established on the previous page</note> [place], they will be **childless** from sexual intercourse <note>original: ζυνουσίας (synousias)</note>. If it is on the **private parts** <note>original: φυσῑκόν (physikon)</note>, the man will be a **begetter of males** <note>original: ἀρρενόγονος (arrenogonos)</note>, but the woman the opposite <note>implying she will give birth to daughters</note>. If it is on the **thigh**, they will be **rich**. If it is on the **knees**, he will take a **rich wife**; as for the woman, if it is on the **right knee**, she will be **good**, but if on the **left**, she will have **many children** <note>original: πολύτεκνος (polyteknos)</note>. If the man has one on the **ankle**, [it is] <unclear>secret</unclear>, [acting] from his **clothing** <note>original: χιτωνίας (chitōnias); perhaps implying modesty or habits related to dress</note>. As for the woman, she will take a **foreign man** from sexual intercourse. If they have a mole on the **loins** <note>original: ψύας (psyas); the muscles of the lower back and flanks</note>, they will have many children.

<margin>Note</margin> Observe then regarding men and women: if the mole is on the **right side**, they will become **rich** and **good in all things**. But if it is on the **left side** <note>original: εὐώνυμα (euōnyma); literally "well-named," a classic Greek euphemism used to avoid saying the "unlucky" left side directly</note>, they will be **passionate** and **poor**.

->~ THE END CONCERNING MOLES ~<-

<note>Vocabulary: mole/olive, begetter of males, having many children, garment/tunic, sexual union, loins, left side, the poor</note>

<summary>This page concludes a treatise on moleosophy, linking moles on the genitals, thighs, and knees to fertility, wealth, and the character of one's spouse. It ends with a definitive rule: moles on the right side of the body signify prosperity, while those on the left signify poverty and passion.</summary>

# C O R R E C T I O N S
<note>original: "ΕΠΑΝΟΡΘΩΜΑΤΑ" (Epanorthōmata). In the early modern period, printing was a manual process prone to error; these lists allowed readers to manually fix mistakes in their personal copies.</note>

<note>The inclusion of the full Greek alphabet at the bottom of an errata page often served as a reference for the font used in the book or as a filler to complete the page's layout.</note>

# C O R R E C T I O N S
<note>original: "ΕΠΑΝΟΡΘΩΜΑΤΑ" (Epanorthōmata). In the early modern period, printing was a manual process prone to error; these lists allowed readers to manually fix mistakes in their personal copies by hand.</note>

->GOOD<-

->S U C C E S S   G O O D<-
<note>The Latin text "EVENTUS BONUS" translates to "Good Success" or "Good Outcome." This was a common printer's mark invoking a prosperous reception for the book.</note>

->S P I R I T<-
<note>The Greek text "ΑΓΑΘΟΣ ΔΑΙΜΩΝ" (Agathos Daimon) refers to a "Good Spirit" or "Noble Divinity." In classical iconography, this figure was associated with protection and good fortune.</note>

# Rome, 1545.
# In the month of January.
